# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

---
name: llp-orient
description: Before editing a subsystem, file, or starting a task, assemble the governing LLP context — read the root LLP, follow the @ref annotations on the code in scope, and find the LLPs covering the relevant systems. Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.
source: ccheever/llp@v0.5.2
---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

  2568	## 22. Wave 12 authorization recipes and open language decisions (RULES2)
  2569	
  2570	The rule-recipes card compiles and executes day/window roster access,
  2571	column-level privacy via public identity/private sheet tables, wider status
  2572	counts and readable role queries, mutable-parent audiences, minimum-group
  2573	publication, secret ballots with closed publication, expiring media capability
  2574	grants, enrolment-scoped ephemeral presence and system-job blind comparisons.
  2575	Invite, self-join and public metadata are separate enrolment models. Trusted
  2576	writers maintain a materialised publication in the same commit as its deciding
  2577	state; a separate repair job cannot be the revocation boundary.
  2578	
  2579	Four decisions remain open; their end games and current disposition are:
  2580	
  2581	- **Column-level read rules:** a field has its own declared authority, with
  2582	  unavailable fields represented explicitly in generated results, indexing,
  2583	  aggregates, downloads and device projections. Do not implement here: masking
  2584	  a shape is insufficient, and changing only serialization leaks through filters
  2585	  and derived data. Public identity/private sheet is the current row-authority
  2586	  recipe, not the primitive. Field-write authority is independent.
  2587	- **Roster plus one group sync union:** deliver both audiences with arm-tagged
  2588	  scope identities, independently revoke one arm while preserving another,
  2589	  acquire both arms with epoch-safe snapshots and prove coverage on both stores.
  2590	  Do not relax the checker here: the empty-prefix roster is already the one
  2591	  group arm and current R2 custody cannot represent a second. Merge roles into
  2592	  one roster or remain online. LLP 3000.036.000.000 §8 owns this protocol work.
  2593	- **Windowed union horizons:** per-arm acquired floors and coverage certificates
  2594	  survive moves, expiry, leave/rejoin, snapshots and pagination without emitting
  2595	  never-authorized IDs. Do not implement here: the prior expiry/sweep ID-disclosure
  2596	  counterexample caused the explicit narrowing; all relevant device/simulator
  2597	  and differential traces must precede reopening LLP 3000.036.000.000 §8.9.
  2598	  Query pagination remains supported; it is not an acquisition window.
  2599	- **changed-only(field):** schema-aware transition sugar expands to equality
  2600	  of every other writable field between old and next, with a documented policy
  2601	  for added columns, field-owned writes and system-maintained fields. Do not
  2602	  add syntax here: the expansion's evolving-schema policy is the unresolved
  2603	  part, not parsing. Explicit old/next equalities are sound today. Language
  2604	  design belongs with the rule grammar, not a permissive mutation require.
  2605	
  2606	Diagnostic contradiction notes are specified in LLP 3000.036.002 §9. The
  2607	refusals ladder shows client codes per stage while preserving authored statement
  2608	order; a nullable hidden lookup is null, an asserted hidden lookup uses its
  2609	assertion code (conventionally NOT_FOUND), and a readable denied write is E_RULE.
  2610	Transport authentication guidance and the rest of client text belong to SURF;
  2611	proof-kit/testing authoring belongs to KIT, except the hidden-only page pointer.
  2612	
  2613	The historical 400-line whole-card test is replaced by a 120-line budget for
  2614	each individually named recipe section. Twenty-one self-contained compiled
  2615	blocks cannot fit the original four-pattern whole-book budget without dropping
  2616	forms or hiding their context. `guide rule-recipes --section` keeps each repair
  2617	bounded and discoverable; the README's 320-line budget and all why/start-card
  2618	budgets are unchanged. No engine/runtime budget is raised.
  2619	
  2620	The presence recipe copies only the stable space/principal audience. Its unique
  2621	`exists enrolments[.space, viewer]` table-rule probe verifies that scope on every
  2622	insert/update. A heartbeat has no read-derived payload or returned row, so it
  2623	qualifies for SDK renewal. Storing an immutable enrolment-row id would couple
  2624	renewal to a replaced membership identity; the stable scope instead renews
  2625	immediately after leave/rejoin. Losing viewer membership hides presence on the
  2626	next read; departed writers' memory rows last at most the declared four seconds.
  2627	The engine's ephemeral semantics are unchanged. The recipe regression checks
  2628	`renews` and rejoining under a new durable enrolment id before the old memory
  2629	row expires.
   640	  p = polls[poll] ! else NOT_FOUND
   641	  require p.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER
   642	  update polls[p.id] { closed: true }
   643	  return null
   644	query ballotResult(poll: polls, choice: enum('yes', 'no')):
   645	  return { count: ballotResults[poll, choice] }
   646	query ownBallot(poll: polls):
   647	  return ballots[poll, viewer]
   648	```
   649	
   650	## Ephemeral presence under an enrolment parent
   651	
   652	An ephemeral row cannot use `read through`. Copy the durable scope, verify it
   653	against the unique enrolment key on every write, and probe the durable
   654	roster for current read authority. Removing a viewer's enrolment hides presence
   655	from that viewer on the next read; a departed writer's row lasts at most 4s.
   656	Renewals require membership, including after leave/rejoin. Never replace presence with a durable table to repair this refusal.
   657	
   658	```quarry
   659	table spaces:
   660	  title: text 1..80
   661	  public 'space metadata is public; participation is private'
   662	  online only
   663	table enrolments:
   664	  space: spaces
   665	  principal: principal
   666	  unique space, principal
   667	  immutable space, principal
   668	  read <- .principal = viewer
   669	  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
   670	  delete <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
   671	  online only
   672	table presenceRows:
   673	  space: spaces
   674	  principal: principal
   675	  unique space, principal
   676	  immutable space, principal
   677	  read <- exists enrolments[.space, viewer]
   678	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists enrolments[.space, viewer]
   679	  update <- .principal = viewer and exists enrolments[.space, viewer]
   680	  ephemeral 4s
   681	mutation pulse(space: spaces):
   682	  upsert presenceRows[space, viewer] {}
   683	mutation leaveSpace(space: spaces):
   684	  delete enrolments[space, viewer]
   685	  return null
   686	query presence(space: spaces, who: principal):
   687	  return presenceRows[space, who]
   688	```
   689	
   690	## Blind comparison with a system job
   691	
   692	A mutation cannot scan or compare secrets its caller cannot read. A system job
   693	can read them, but must publish only the explicitly permitted derived fact.
   694	Here the operator schedules the comparison, and ordinary callers get only
   695	which of two known submissions is higher, not either score. Jobs run as the
   696	system; no client mutation delegates arbitrary secret comparisons.
   697	
   698	```quarry
   699	table secretScores:
   700	  owner: principal
   701	  score: int
   702	  immutable owner, score
   703	  read <- .owner = viewer
   704	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   705	  online only
   706	table comparisons:
   707	  left: secretScores
   708	  right: secretScores
   709	  leftHigher: bool
   710	  public 'operator-authorized comparison discloses only the ordering'

    33	            (
    34	                k.clone(),
    35	                wire::from_json(t, args.get(k).unwrap_or(&Json::Null)).unwrap(),
    36	            )
    37	        })
    38	        .collect();
    39	    Context::new(who, args).at(now)
    40	}
    41	fn write(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, who: &str, args: Json, now: i64) -> Json {
    42	    let c = ctx(e, op, who, args, now);
    43	    match e.mutate(op, None, c).unwrap().state {
    44	        WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => wire::to_json(&result),
    45	        other => panic!("{op}: {other:?}"),
    46	    }
    47	}
    48	fn fail(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, who: &str, args: Json, now: i64, code: &str) {
    49	    let c = ctx(e, op, who, args, now);
    50	    let state = e.mutate(op, None, c).unwrap().state;
    51	    assert!(
    52	        matches!(&state, WriteState::Failed { why, .. } if why.code == code),
    53	        "{op}: expected {code}, got {state:?}"
    54	    );
    55	}
    56	
    57	fn read(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, who: &str, args: Json, now: i64) -> Json {
    58	    let c = ctx(e, op, who, args, now);
    59	    wire::to_json(&e.query(op, c).unwrap().data)
    60	}
    61	fn seed(e: &mut Engine, table: &str, data: Json) {
    62	    let row = wire::row_from_json(e.schema().table(table).unwrap(), &data).unwrap();
    63	    e.seed_at(vec![(table.into(), row)], 0).unwrap();
    64	}

     1	# Ephemeral tables: typing and presence in a room
     2	
     3	Ephemeral tables hold short-lived rows in one server process, under ordinary table rules. Use them for typing and viewing indicators that disappear without durable writes.
     4	
     5	```quarry
     6	table rooms:
     7	  name: text <=80
     8	  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]
     9	  sync to members[.id]
    10	
    11	table members:
    12	  roomId: rooms
    13	  principal: principal
    14	  unique roomId, principal
    15	  by byPrincipal: principal, roomId
    16	  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    17	  sync to members[.roomId] last 100 by .id
    18	
    19	table typing:
    20	  roomId: rooms
    21	  principal: principal
    22	  unique roomId, principal
    23	  cap 50 by roomId
    24	  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    25	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    26	  update <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    27	  delete <- .principal = viewer
    28	  ephemeral 4s
    29	
    30	mutation setTyping(roomId: rooms):
    31	  upsert typing[roomId, viewer] {}
    32	
    33	mutation stopTyping(roomId: rooms):
    34	  delete typing[roomId, viewer]
    35	
    36	query typingIn(roomId: rooms):
    37	  return typing where .roomId = roomId and .principal != viewer by .principal { principal }
    38	
    39	query typingInMyRooms(c: cursor ?):
    40	  return members where .principal = viewer first 100 by .roomId after c { roomId, typing: typing[roomId = .roomId] where .principal != viewer { principal } }
    41	
    42	table viewing:
    43	  roomId: rooms
    44	  principal: principal
    45	  unique roomId, principal
    46	  cap 50 by roomId
    47	  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    48	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    49	  update <- .principal = viewer and exists members[.roomId, viewer]
    50	  delete <- .principal = viewer
    51	  ephemeral 30s
    52	
    53	mutation markViewing(roomId: rooms):
    54	  upsert viewing[roomId, viewer] {}
    55	
    56	mutation stopViewing(roomId: rooms):
    57	  delete viewing[roomId, viewer]
    58	
    59	query viewingIn(roomId: rooms):
    60	  return viewing where .roomId = roomId by .principal { principal }
    61	```
    62	
    63	Keep the ordinary chats-list query device-held. Observe `typingInMyRooms`
    64	separately with `{ keep: true }` and render its room hints beside that list: never join an
    65	ephemeral table into a query that must work offline.
    66	A query reading ephemeral rows runs on the server; `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`
    67	explains this and suggests splitting it from device-held data.
    68	
    69	## Calling from React
    70	
    71	```tsx
    72	const typing = useQuery(api.typingIn, { roomId }, { keep: true });
    73	const setTyping = useMutation(api.setTyping);
    74	const stopTyping = useMutation(api.stopTyping);
    75	<Composer onChangeText={text => text ? setTyping.run({ roomId }) : stopTyping.run({ roomId })}
    76	          onSend={() => stopTyping.run({ roomId })} />;
    77	<TypingIndicator visible={"data" in typing && (typing.data?.length ?? 0) > 0} />;
    78	```
    79	
    80	`keep: true` keeps the indicator during a re-read after an unrelated write;
    81	expiry still clears it.
    82	A kept answer may show rows after membership is revoked until the re-read lands;
    83	keeping is an application choice (`guide reads`).
    84	`useQuery` watches normally and re-reads at the row's expiry. The client sends
    85	calls in order. `setTyping` is marked `renews`: waiting equal calls coalesce,
    86	and an equal last successful call can be reused for one quarter of the life
    87	when nothing intervenes. Deletes and inserts are never coalesced. A row
    88	expires between `0.75 × life` and `life` after the last call, plus delivery;
    89	for a 5 s bound use `ephemeral 4s` or less.
    90	
    91	A bare ephemeral write returns `null`. Use explicit `return` when you need
    92	a result; that operation never coalesces. `renews` requires exactly one final
    93	upsert or update, with argument/viewer/literal/`now` keys and patch values,
    94	preceded only by guards or their local values. Counters and toggles do not renew.
    95	
    96	For "who has this room open", keep a viewing heartbeat while mounted:
    97	
    98	```tsx
    99	const markViewing = useMutation(api.markViewing);
   100	const stopViewing = useMutation(api.stopViewing);
   101	useEffect(() => {
   102	  void markViewing.run({ roomId });
   103	  const heartbeat = setInterval(() => { void markViewing.run({ roomId }); }, 10_000);
   104	  return () => { clearInterval(heartbeat); void stopViewing.run({ roomId }); };
   105	}, [roomId, markViewing.run, stopViewing.run]);
   106	```
   107	
   108	Import `useQuery` and `useMutation` from `snapback4/react`, and `useEffect`
   109	from React. The generated `api` supplies the operation handles. Calls resolve
   110	to `Signal<R>` (`sent` or `failed`); offline calls fail with `E_OFFLINE` and
   111	are dropped, never held, retried or added to an outbox. A server refusal or a
   112	malformed reply (`E_HTTP_RESPONSE`, with HTTP status) keeps the link online and
   113	waiting calls in order. Neither replays the failed call. A suspended or disconnected viewer disappears
   114	within its 30 s life without cleanup succeeding.
   115	
   116	## Life and the renewal law
   117	
   118	Life is required, from 1 s through 60 s. Every insert, update and upsert
   119	stamps a hidden deadline at the server's current time plus the life; even an
   120	empty update renews it. Every deadline-stamping write must pass the insert
   121	rule on its new image as well as its ordinary write rule. This renewal law
   122	means a removed member cannot keep a row alive past one life, even if an
   123	update rule checks only ownership. Keep membership in both rules as shown.
   124	
   125	Rows are absent at their deadline, before rules run. Deadlines are hidden
   126	from returned rows; query answers carry `nextExpiry`. `connected[p]` remains
   127	server-wide; it does not report who is viewing a room. Memory is cleared on
   128	restart, adoption, generation change or store replacement. One server
   129	process serves these rows; there is no multi-server presence.
   130	
   131	## Refused declarations and uses
   132	
   133	E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL refuses a declaration or operation that gives ephemeral rows durable custody. Use life from 1 s to 60 s, remove sync and durable clauses, and split mixed writes into separate client calls. Every row below names the clause and fix.
   134	
   135	| Clause or use | Fix |
   136	| --- | --- |
   137	| Missing life, below 1 s or above 60 s | Use `ephemeral 4s` |
   138	| `sync`, `sync public`, a horizon, `online only` | Delete the line; devices read these rows from the server |
   139	| `expire at .col` | Delete it; the declaration sets every deadline |
   140	| `sequence by`, `through`, `words(..)`, `image`, `video`, `once` | Keep that data in a durable table |
   141	| Source of a maintain, Following view or feed | Count or rank durable rows |
   142	| Any rule or `after insert` probing an ephemeral table | Probe durable rows instead |
   143	| A reference column or `sync to` targeting an ephemeral table | Keep that target durable |
   144	| A durable mutation, job, action or schedule reading or writing memory | Call a separate ephemeral mutation from the client |
   145	| One mutation writing both durable and ephemeral rows, or emitting an effect | Split it; call the ephemeral mutation after the durable one |
   146	| A `held` query reading memory | Split that read into its own server query |
   147	
   148	Unique keys, table caps and normal work meters apply. Admission charges each
   149	row's declared maximum column and index size, never the private payload's
   150	actual length. `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` is retryable at 1,000 rows or 4 MiB per
     1	//! Wave 10 NA8: complete role probes, blind child writers, and mutable
     2	//! read-through audience guidance. The recipes are the shipped guide blocks.
     3	use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, value::object};
     4	use snapback4_lang::{Bound, compile, guide};
     5	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     6	
     7	fn recipe(needle: &str) -> String {
     8	    guide::quarry(guide::card("rule-recipes").unwrap())
     9	        .into_iter()
    10	        .find(|block| block.contains(needle))
    11	        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("recipes card has no Quarry block containing {needle}"))
    12	}
    13	
    14	fn clean(source: &str) -> Bound {
    15	    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    16	    assert!(
    17	        bound.diagnostics.is_empty(),
    18	        "{:#?}\n{source}",
    19	        bound.diagnostics
    20	    );
    21	    bound
    22	}
    23	
    24	fn id(value: &str) -> Value {
    25	    Value::Id(value.into())
    26	}
    27	fn text(value: &str) -> Value {
    28	    Value::String(value.into())
    29	}
    30	fn context(viewer: &str, args: &[(&str, Value)]) -> Context {
    31	    Context::new(
    32	        viewer,
    33	        args.iter()
    34	            .map(|(name, value)| ((*name).into(), value.clone()))
    35	            .collect(),
    36	    )
    37	}
    38	fn bool_query(engine: &mut Engine, operation: &str, viewer: &str) -> bool {
    39	    match engine
    40	        .query(operation, Context::new(viewer, BTreeMap::new()))
    41	        .unwrap()
    42	        .data
    43	    {
    44	        Value::Bool(value) => value,
    45	        value => panic!("{operation} returned {value:?}"),
    46	    }
    47	}
    48	
    49	#[test]
    50	fn composite_role_recipe_uses_full_probes_and_supports_two_roles() {
    51	    let source = recipe("query canCoordinate():");
    52	    let bound = clean(&source);
    53	    let mut engine = Engine::memory(
    54	        bound.schema,
    55	        bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program),
    56	    )
    57	    .unwrap();
    58	    engine
    59	        .seed([
    60	            (
    61	                "roles".into(),
    62	                object([
    63	                    ("id", id("alice-coordinator")),
    64	                    ("principal", id("alice")),
    65	                    ("role", text("coordinator")),
    66	                ]),
    67	            ),
    68	            (
    69	                "roles".into(),
    70	                object([
    71	                    ("id", id("alice-commander")),
    72	                    ("principal", id("alice")),
    73	                    ("role", text("commander")),
    74	                ]),
    75	            ),
    76	            (
    77	                "roles".into(),
    78	                object([
    79	                    ("id", id("bob-coordinator")),
    80	                    ("principal", id("bob")),
    81	                    ("role", text("coordinator")),
    82	                ]),
    83	            ),
    84	        ])
    85	        .unwrap();
    86	
    87	    assert!(bool_query(&mut engine, "canCoordinate", "alice"));
    88	    assert!(bool_query(&mut engine, "canCommand", "alice"));
    89	    assert!(bool_query(&mut engine, "canCoordinate", "bob"));
    90	    assert!(!bool_query(&mut engine, "canCommand", "bob"));
    91	
    92	    let partial = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('coordinator', 'commander')\n  unique principal, role\n  public 'roles'\n  online only\nquery anyRole():\n  return exists roles[viewer]\n";
    93	    let refused = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), partial.into())]);
    94	    let diagnostic = refused
    95	        .diagnostics
    96	        .iter()
    97	        .find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN")
    98	        .unwrap_or_else(|| {
    99	            panic!(
   100	                "bare composite-key prefix was admitted: {:#?}",
   101	                refused.diagnostics
   102	            )
   103	        });
   104	    assert!(
   105	        diagnostic.message.contains("name the key columns")
   106	            || diagnostic.message.contains("positional key"),
   107	        "{diagnostic:#?}"
   108	    );
   109	}
   110	
   111	#[test]
   112	fn blind_writer_recipe_checks_the_parent_without_reading_it() {
   113	    let source = recipe("mutation addLetter(application:");
   114	    let bound = clean(&source);
   115	    let mut engine = Engine::memory(
   116	        bound.schema,
   117	        bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program),
   118	    )
   119	    .unwrap();
   120	    engine
   121	        .seed([
   122	            (
   123	                "counselors".into(),
   124	                object([("id", id("counselor")), ("principal", id("carol"))]),
   125	            ),
   126	            (
   127	                "applications".into(),
   128	                object([
   129	                    ("id", id("application")),
   130	                    ("student", id("alice")),
   131	                    ("college", text("Example University")),
   132	                ]),
   133	            ),
   134	        ])
   135	        .unwrap();
   136	
   137	    let sent = engine
   138	        .mutate(
   139	            "addLetter",
   140	            None,
   141	            context(
   142	                "bob",
   143	                &[
   144	                    ("application", id("application")),
   145	                    ("body", text("Bob recommends Alice.")),
   146	                ],
   147	            )
   148	            .with_ids(["letter".into()]),
   149	        )
   150	        .unwrap();
   151	    assert!(
   152	        matches!(sent.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }),
   153	        "a blind recommender should write: {sent:?}"
   154	    );
   155	
   156	    for (viewer, application, row_id) in [
   157	        ("alice", "application", "own-letter"),
   158	        ("bob", "missing", "missing-letter"),
   159	    ] {
   160	        let refused = engine
   161	            .mutate(
   162	                "addLetter",
   163	                None,
   164	                context(
   165	                    viewer,
   166	                    &[("application", id(application)), ("body", text("No."))],
   167	                )
   168	                .with_ids([row_id.into()]),
   169	            )
   170	            .unwrap();
   171	        assert!(
   172	            matches!(&refused.state, WriteState::Failed { why, .. } if why.code == "E_RULE"),
   173	            "{viewer}/{application}: {refused:?}"
   174	        );
   175	    }
   176	
   177	    let read = |engine: &mut Engine, viewer| {

   183	                    &[("application", id("application")), ("c", Value::Null)],
   184	                ),
   185	            )
   186	            .unwrap()
   187	            .data
   188	            .as_array()
   189	            .unwrap()
   190	            .len()

The recipe now uses stable space/principal keys, and its bare heartbeat matches the documented renewal form. The access rules and leave/rejoin regression align with the amendment. I’m checking section discovery and the budget guard before the final verdict.

 ## 0. Summary
@@ -2609,3 +2609,21 @@ order; a nullable hidden lookup is null, an asserted hidden lookup uses its
 assertion code (conventionally NOT_FOUND), and a readable denied write is E_RULE.
 Transport authentication guidance and the rest of client text belong to SURF;
 proof-kit/testing authoring belongs to KIT, except the hidden-only page pointer.
+
+The historical 400-line whole-card test is replaced by a 120-line budget for
+each individually named recipe section. Twenty-one self-contained compiled
+blocks cannot fit the original four-pattern whole-book budget without dropping
+forms or hiding their context. `guide rule-recipes --section` keeps each repair
+bounded and discoverable; the README's 320-line budget and all why/start-card
+budgets are unchanged. No engine/runtime budget is raised.
+
+The presence recipe copies only the stable space/principal audience. Its unique
+`exists enrolments[.space, viewer]` table-rule probe verifies that scope on every
+insert/update. A heartbeat has no read-derived payload or returned row, so it
+qualifies for SDK renewal. Storing an immutable enrolment-row id would couple
+renewal to a replaced membership identity; the stable scope instead renews
+immediately after leave/rejoin. Losing viewer membership hides presence on the
+next read; departed writers' memory rows last at most the declared four seconds.
+The engine's ephemeral semantics are unchanged. The recipe regression checks
+`renews` and rejoining under a new durable enrolment id before the old memory
+row expires.

 ## 1. Scope and motivation
@@ -340,3 +340,14 @@ including in if conditions. Their definitions can contain writing helpers;
 walking only a shape's base cannot certify it read-only. Clear all entry facts
 before evaluation and retain none afterwards; do not borrow stale snapshots
 through a named shape or spread. Pure shapes may therefore lose note reach.
+
+### RULES2 review disposition
+
+The amendments in this section and LLP 3000.036 §22, with the implementation
+at `45c2936e64`, received mutually blind READY verdicts from
+[Muse](reviews/3000.036.002-rules2-r3.muse.md) and
+[fresh Codex](reviews/3000.036.002-rules2-r3.codex.md). Verbatim earlier rounds
+remain beside them: their material corrections cover canonical numeric values,
+if-condition effects and opaque shape/spread effects. Qualification also updates
+the device's authoring census with 27 new `never` mutation occurrences from the
+recipe card; every prior occurrence and eligible private write site is preserved.
8:**Revised:** 2026-10-07 (§3.4: release the hub during SQLite adoption; LLP 3000.033 refusal contract); 2026-09-28 (§2.4: preserve HTTP admission refusals without replay); 2026-09-26 (implemented by lanes E1, E2 and E3; three blind code reviews of `1d6325c86`, Grok READY TO LAND and Astra and Opus BLOCKED, then fix round 1 green; §8); 2026-09-26 (revision 3, after Astra's READY WITH CHANGES delta check of revision 2: a conservative `renews` proof, wake publication under the hub lock, an ephemeral request revision that retires old incarnations, a 1 s refresh of incomplete ephemeral answers; §8); 2026-09-26 (revision 2, after three blind reviews — Opus and Grok READY WITH CHANGES, Astra NOT READY: static ephemeral read sets and poll re-issue, a lost-wakeup-free wake mutex, per-watch incarnation/epoch fencing, a generation fence on the hub, an exhaustive effect classifier with a runtime boundary, the renewal law, declared-size per-principal capacity, one ordered client lane with `renews`-only coalescing, disjoint mutation types, no rule probes of ephemeral tables; §8); 2026-09-26 (revision 1); 2026-09-27 (FK: expired local server replies recover with paced retries)
52:lane per client. A renewal such as `setTyping` coalesces with an identical
137:  coalescing and "offline publishes … never enter the durable mutation
190:### 2.2 Ephemeral mutations
213:The binder also marks an ephemeral mutation **`renews`** only when it can
227:`setTyping` renews. None of these does: `stopTyping`, an insert of a
231:renewal.) Only `renews` operations are coalesced or suppressed by the
236:- `check`'s program listing, as `ephemeral` or `ephemeral, renews`;
245:  existing row, stamps it again, even with an empty patch. That renewal is
249:- **The renewal law.** Any write that stamps `$deadline` must pass the
312:- `renews: true` when the binder marked it;
313:- `renewMs`: a quarter of the shortest life among the tables it writes, in
324:  { kind: "mutation", name: "setTyping", runs: "server", predicts: "never", ephemeral: true, renews: true, renewMs: 1000 };
335:  readonly renews?: true; readonly renewMs: number };
360:1. **Coalescing, `renews` operations only.**
362:   - A new `renews` call whose key matches a call still waiting in the lane
366:   - Non-`renews` operations are never coalesced.
367:2. **Renewal window, `renews` operations only.**

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

     1	# Rule recipes: audiences, disclosure and blind writers
     2	
     3	These compiled recipes express roles, copied audiences, consent, publication, link enrollment and anonymity. Each makes the intended disclosure and write authority explicit, including the trade-offs of materialising a grant.
     4	
     5	```quarry
     6	use personas alice, bob, carol
     7	
     8	table roles:
     9	  principal: principal
    10	  role: enum('coordinator', 'commander')
    11	  unique principal, role
    12	  read <- .principal = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'commander']
    13	  insert <- exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'commander'] or viewer = 'service:ops'
    14	  delete <- exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'commander']
    15	  online only
    16	
    17	table incidents:
    18	  owner: principal
    19	  title: text 1..120
    20	  immutable owner
    21	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'coordinator'] or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'commander']
    22	  insert <- .owner = viewer
    23	  update <- exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'coordinator'] or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'commander']
    24	  online only
    25	
    26	query canCoordinate():
   835	  return insert mediaLinks { document: d.id, secret, expiresAt }
   836	mutation claimMedia(secret: text 32..100):
   837	  t = mediaLinks[secret = secret] ! else BAD_LINK
   838	  return insert mediaReaders { document: t.document, principal: viewer, expiresAt: t.expiresAt }
   839	mutation revokeMediaLink(secret: text 32..100):
   840	  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
   841	  t = mediaLinks[secret = secret] ! else BAD_LINK
   842	  delete mediaLinks[t.id]
   843	  return null
   844	mutation revokeMediaReader(document: mediaDocuments, who: principal):
   845	  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
   846	  delete mediaReaders[document = document, principal = who]
   847	  return null
   848	query mediaDocumentById(document: mediaDocuments):
   849	  return mediaDocuments[document]
   850	```
   851	
   852	Only expose claimMedia as the reader writer. A generic insert of a grant would
   853	bypass possession and expiry checks. Repeated claims use the declared unique
   854	constraint; they do not extend a grant's expiry.
   855	
   856	## N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE
   857	
   858	N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE means a nullable point lookup is always null for callers
   859	satisfying its preceding require/branch conditions: those conditions contradict
   860	the table read rule, so a non-null branch cannot run. Authorize the read or
   861	write by key under the table rule; Enrolment into hidden things shows the latter.
   862	Unknown relationships and role overlap do not trigger this proof.
   863	
   864	## Self-join through a public catalogue
   865	
   866	The public catalogue is the root metadata; the private payload has a separate
   867	row rule. Open entries permit self-join without an invitation. Closed entries
   868	refuse the join even though their metadata is readable. Opening an entry is
   869	an explicit owner decision, not a consequence of knowing its id.
   870	
   871	```quarry
   872	table clubCatalogue:
   873	  owner: principal
   874	  title: text 1..80
   875	  open: bool
   876	  immutable owner
   877	  public 'club titles and whether enrolment is open are public metadata'
   878	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   879	  update <- .owner = viewer
   880	  online only
   881	table catalogueMembers:
   882	  club: clubCatalogue
   883	  principal: principal
   884	  unique club, principal
   885	  immutable club, principal
   886	  read <- .principal = viewer
   887	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists clubCatalogue[.club, open = true]
   888	  online only
   889	table clubSheets:
   890	  club: clubCatalogue
   891	  owner: principal
   892	  body: text 1..200
   893	  unique club
   894	  immutable club, owner
   895	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists catalogueMembers[.club, viewer]
   896	  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists clubCatalogue[.club, owner = .owner]
   897	  online only
   898	mutation catalogueClub(title: text 1..80, open: bool, body: text 1..200):
   899	  c = insert clubCatalogue { owner: viewer, title, open }
   900	  insert clubSheets { club: c.id, owner: viewer, body }
   901	  return c
   902	mutation selfJoinClub(club: clubCatalogue):
   903	  return insert catalogueMembers { club, principal: viewer }
   904	query catalogueClubById(club: clubCatalogue):
   905	  return clubCatalogue[club]
   906	query clubSheet(club: clubCatalogue):
   907	  return clubSheets[club = club]
   908	```

   179	/// The contents of every ```quarry block in a card or a README.
   180	pub fn quarry(text: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   181	    let mut blocks = Vec::new();
   182	    let mut current: Option<String> = None;
   183	    for line in text.lines() {
   184	        match &mut current {
   185	            Some(block) if line.trim_end() == "```" => {
   186	                blocks.push(std::mem::take(block));
   187	                current = None;
   188	            }
   189	            Some(block) => {
   190	                block.push_str(line);
   191	                block.push('\n');
   192	            }
   193	            None if line.trim_end() == "```quarry" => current = Some(String::new()),
   194	            None => {}
   195	        }
   196	    }
   197	    blocks
   198	}

    16	#[test]
    17	fn time_waiting_cards_point_to_cli_and_http_clock_controls() {
    18	    for name in ["expire", "jobs", "effects"] {
    19	        let card = snapback4_lang::guide::card(name).unwrap();
    20	        assert!(card.contains("snapback4 clock advance <dur>"), "{name}: missing test pointer");
    21	        assert!(card.contains("POST /dev/clock/advance"), "{name}: missing HTTP pointer");
    22	        assert!(card.contains("snapback4 clock advance 36s\ncurl"), "{name}: missing two-line example");
    23	    }
    24	    assert!(snapback4_lang::guide::card("presence").unwrap().contains("In tests: `snapback4 clock advance 36s`"));
    25	    let readme = README;
    26	    assert!(readme.contains("snapback4 clock advance <dur>"));
    27	    assert!(readme.contains("snapback4 clock advance 36s\n  curl"));
    28	}
    29	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

    31	const README: &str = concat!(include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md"), "\n", include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md"), "\n", include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/SERVER.md"));
    32	
    33	// A relocated assertion checks the actual orientation door as well as its destination.
    34	fn readme_reference(name: &str) -> &'static str {
    35	    let map = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md").split_once("## Reference map").unwrap().1;
    36	    assert!(map.contains(&format!("`guide {name}`")), "orientation omits guide {name}");
    37	    card(name).unwrap()
    38	}

    40	#[test]
    41	fn feeds_card_states_background_contract_and_keeps_recipe_refusals() {
    42	    let feeds = card("feeds").unwrap();
    43	    assert_eq!(feeds, readme_reference("feeds"));
    44	    let contract = feeds.split_once("## Following contract").unwrap().1.split_once("## Boundaries").unwrap().0
    45	        .split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    46	    for fact in ["newest first", "Your own events appear immediately", "normally within a second", "copies their newest 50 live events immediately, then the rest of a 1,000-event window", "Older posts remain in `posts` and are not copied.", "Unfollow hides that actor on the next read", "your backfill or cleanup is pending", "Removal hides the event immediately", "without prediction"] {
    47	        assert!(contract.contains(fact), "missing {fact}");
    48	    }
    49	    for bad in ["skips a follower", "skip saturated readers", "A skipped follower", "not skipped\nauthors"] {
    50	        assert!(!feeds.contains(bad), "{bad}");
    51	    }
    52	    for code in ["FEED_PULL_CAP", "FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", "E_RECIPE_RETIRED"] {
    53	        assert_eq!(snapback4_lang::diag::guide_for(code), "feeds");
    54	        assert!(explain_code(code).unwrap().contains("snapback4 guide feeds"));
    55	        assert!(codes().contains(&code));
    56	    }
    57	    for (code, facts) in [
    58	        ("E_RECIPE_RETIRED", vec!["view following: activity", "the engine chooses delivery bounds"]),
    59	        ("E_TIMELINE_FILTER", vec!["view activity", "posts where .parentId = null and not .removed"]),
    60	        ("E_TIMELINE_READ", vec!["following last 20 by .at after c"]),
    61	        ("E_TIMELINE_ACTIVATE", vec!["query home(c: cursor ?): return activity where .actor = viewer or exists follows[viewer, .actor] last 20 by .at after c", "Original event ids are post ids", "--allow-feed-adoption", "5,000,000 copies"]),
    62	        ("FEED_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared authors", "rolled back", "new write ID"]),
    63	        ("FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared-author slots", "rolled back", "public", "pullCounts", "server-run query", "used = 32"]),
    64	    ] {
    65	        let explanation = explain_code(code).unwrap();
  1090	}

  1092	// Wave 6 (LLP 3000.007 §20.1): `guide reads --section pagination` exited 1 and
  1093	// `guide recipes --find capacity` found nothing although both are aliases of
  1094	// those cards (074, 086). Every alias now works inside its own card, and the
  1095	// curated ones land on the section they name.
  1096	#[test]
  1097	fn every_alias_is_a_section_or_find_word_inside_its_card() {
  1098	    for &(word, target) in guide::ALIASES {
  1099	        let text = card(target).unwrap();
  1100	        assert!(guide::section(target, text, word).is_ok(), "guide {target} --section {word:?} refuses");
  1101	        assert!(guide::find(target, text, word).is_ok(), "guide {target} --find {word:?} refuses");
  1102	    }
  1103	    for &(word, heading) in guide::ALIAS_SECTIONS {
  1104	        let (target, _) = guide::alias_section(word).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{word} is not an alias"));
  1105	        let text = card(target).unwrap();
  1106	        let landing = guide::alias_landing(word, target, text).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{word}: no heading of guide {target} starts with {heading:?}"));
  1107	        let title = text.lines().next().unwrap();
  1108	        assert!(landing.starts_with(&format!("{title}\n({word} -> guide {target}, section ")), "{landing}");
  1109	        assert!(landing.lines().nth(3).is_some_and(|line| line.starts_with("##") && line.contains(heading.split(' ').next().unwrap())), "{word}: {landing}");
  1110	        assert_eq!(guide::section(target, text, word).unwrap(), landing, "{word}: --section lands where the bare alias does");
  1111	        assert!(landing.lines().count() < text.lines().count(), "{word} prints one section");
  1112	    }
  1113	    let reads = card("reads").unwrap();
  1114	    let pages = guide::alias_landing("pagination", "reads", reads).unwrap();
  1115	    assert!(pages.contains("### Pages and cursors") && pages.contains("`c: cursor ?`") && pages.contains("`next`") && pages.contains("usePage"), "{pages}");
  1116	    let capacity = guide::find("recipes", card("recipes").unwrap(), "capacity").unwrap();
  1117	    assert!(capacity.contains("## Booking"), "{capacity}");
  1118	    assert_eq!(card("aggregates"), card("totals"), "100 looked for totals under `aggregates`");
  1119	    // A whole-card alias that no line names prints the opening, never a miss.
  1120	    let oracle = guide::section("read-only", card("read-only").unwrap(), "oracle").unwrap();
  1121	    assert!(oracle.starts_with("oracle is an alias of the whole guide read-only card") && oracle.contains("# Read-only"), "{oracle}");
  1122	    // Words that are neither an alias nor in the card still refuse.
  1123	    assert!(guide::section("reads", reads, "banana").is_err() && guide::find("reads", reads, "banana").is_err());
  1124	}
  1125	
  1126	// Wave 6: the bare listing ended in one ~5 KB line of aliases (098); it is one
  1127	// screen now, and `guide aliases` lists them one per line.
  1128	#[test]
  1129	fn the_listing_fits_a_screen_and_aliases_have_their_own_command() {
  1130	    let listing = guide::listing();
  1131	    assert!(listing.lines().count() <= 10 && listing.lines().all(|line| line.len() < 600), "{listing}");
  1132	    assert!(listing.contains("snapback4 guide aliases") && !listing.contains("offline -> prediction"), "{listing}");
  1133	    let aliases = guide::aliases();
  1134	    assert_eq!(aliases.lines().count(), guide::ALIASES.len() + 1);
  1135	    for &(word, target) in guide::ALIASES { assert!(aliases.lines().any(|line| line.starts_with(&format!("{word} -> {target}"))), "{word}"); }
  1136	    assert!(aliases.contains("pagination -> reads --section \"Pages and cursors\""), "{aliases}");
  1137	}

  1142	#[test]
  1143	fn a_piped_overview_carries_the_first_section_and_the_terminal_one_the_index() {
  1144	    for name in KEYWORDS {

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/lowering/r1p-sources.jsonl:367:["8144847c320d598434520f9473e54dd876de64303e93988fc10e0c0ff88d44d9",[["schema.q","-- The chat backend as Snapback 4 wants to read it. This file is the desired\n-- authoring surface (LLP 2000 §4: write the application in the interface we\n-- wish to provide); nothing compiles it until week 2. It is the Snapback 2\n-- chat schema with one word changed per table: `sync` (LLP 2000.000 §7.1)\n-- says which rows reach a viewer's device and how far back. Everything the\n-- certifier keeps — rules, indexes, caps, `deliver`'s audience computation —\n-- is unchanged in meaning.\n\nuse identity\nuse personas alice, bob, charlie\n\naction invite(email: text <=200)\n\ntable profiles:\n  principal: principal\n  displayName: text 1..50\n  handle: text 3..20 format handle\n  avatarUrl: text <=2048 format url ?\n  unique byPrincipal: principal\n  unique byHandle: handle\n  immutable principal\n  public 'profiles are public'\n  insert <- .principal = viewer\n  update <- .principal = viewer\n  delete <- deny\n  sync last 2000 by byHandle        -- everyone holds the same bounded partition\n\ntable conversations:\n  title: text <=120\n  creatorId: principal\n  read <- exists memberships[.id, viewer]\n  insert <- .creatorId = viewer\n  update <- deny\n  delete <- deny\n  after insert <- exists memberships[conversationId = .id, principal = .creatorId, role = 'admin']\n  sync by .principal                 -- a member's device holds its conversations\n\ntable memberships:\n  conversationId: conversations\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('member', 'admin')\n  invitedBy: principal\n  joinedAt: time\n  unique byConversationPrincipal: conversationId, principal\n  by byConversationPrincipalRole: conversationId, principal, role\n  by byPrincipalConversation: principal, conversationId, id\n  cap 50 by conversationId\n  immutable conversationId, principal, joinedAt\n  read <- exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]\n  insert <- exists memberships[conversationId = .conversationId, principal = viewer, role = 'admin']\n  delete <- .principal = viewer or exists memberships[conversationId = .conversationId, principal = viewer, role = 'admin']\n  sync by .principal     -- and the roster of each\n\ntable messages:\n  conversationId: conversations\n  authorId: principal\n  body: text <=4000 ?\n  photo: image <=5000000 ?\n  editedAt: time ?\n  at: time\n  by byConvTime: conversationId, at, id\n  immutable conversationId, authorId\n  read <- exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]\n  insert <- .authorId = viewer and exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]\n  update <- .authorId = viewer\n  delete <- .authorId = viewer\n  retain delivered-history                 -- an expelled member keeps what was delivered\n  sync by .invitedBy last 500 by byConvTime   -- the horizon: 500 per conversation\n\nmaintain messageCounts: count messages by conversationId\n  read <- exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]\n  -- delivered into the partition as a value row: a count is a local fact\n\nquery thread(conversationId: conversations, c: cursor ?):\n  require exists memberships[conversationId, viewer] else MEMBERSHIP_REQUIRED\n  return messages[conversationId] after c last 50 by byConvTime\n\nquery inbox():\n  return memberships[viewer] first 50 by byPrincipalConversation\n    { conversationId, conversation: conversations[.conversationId], count: messageCounts[.conversationId] }\n\nmutation addMember(conversationId: conversations, principal: principal):\n  require exists memberships[conversationId = conversationId, principal = viewer, role = 'admin'] by byConversationPrincipalRole else NOT_ADMIN\n  return insert memberships { conversationId, principal, invitedBy: viewer, role: 'member', joinedAt: now } if absent\n\nmutation leave(conversationId: conversations):\n  membership = memberships[conversationId, viewer] ! else NOT_MEMBER\n  delete memberships[membership]\n  return null\n\nmutation send(conversationId: conversations, body: text <=4000):\n  require exists memberships[conversationId, viewer] else NOT_MEMBER\n  row = insert messages { conversationId, authorId: viewer, body, at: now }\n  return { id: row.id }\n\nmutation sendPhoto(conversationId: conversations, photo: image <=5000000, body: text <=4000 ?):\n  require exists memberships[conversationId, viewer] else NOT_MEMBER\n  row = insert messages { conversationId, authorId: viewer, body, photo, at: now }\n  return { id: row.id }\n\nmutation edit(messageId: messages, body: text <=4000):\n  msg = messages[messageId] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require msg.authorId = viewer else NOT_AUTHOR\n  update messages[msg] { body, editedAt: now }\n  return null\n\nmutation unsend(messageId: messages):\n  msg = messages[messageId] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require msg.authorId = viewer else NOT_AUTHOR\n  delete messages[msg]\n  return null\n"]]]
